<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heat will liquefy the butter in about two minutes.  加热约两分钟就能把黄油化成液体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>industrious</b></td><td>/ɪnˈdʌstriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勤勉的，刻苦的</td><td>an industrious worker<br>勤劳的工人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The most industrious student in the class reviewed her notes every single night.  班里最勤奋的学生每晚都复习笔记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assiduous</td><td>/əˈsɪdʒuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勤勉的，专心仔细的</td><td>assiduous attention to detail<br>对细节的专注仔细</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her assiduous attention to detail saved the project from disaster.  她对细节的专注仔细使项目免于失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 busy</td><td>/ˈbɪzi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忙碌的，繁忙的</td><td>a busy intersection<br>繁忙的路口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctors were too busy to answer any of our questions.  医生们太忙了，无法回答我们任何问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diligent</td><td>/ˈdɪlɪdʒənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>勤勉的，辛勤的</td><td>a diligent student<br>勤奋的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A diligent editor checked every footnote before the manuscript went to the printer.  一位勤勉的编辑在书稿付印前核对了每一条脚注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 sedulous</del></td><td>/ˈsedʒələs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勤奋认真的</td><td>sedulous preparation<br>勤勉认真的准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sedulous preparation showed in every answer she gave the examiners.  她勤勉认真的准备体现在给考官的每个回答中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indolent</td><td>/ˈɪndələnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>懒惰的</td><td>an indolent young man<br>懒散的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too indolent to look for work, he simply lay on the couch all summer.  他懒得去找工作，整个夏天就躺在沙发上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lazy</td><td>/ˈleɪzi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>懒惰的</td><td>too lazy to cook<br>懒得做饭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was too lazy to walk the dog on cold mornings.  寒冷的早晨他懒得去遛狗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slothful</td><td>/ˈsloʊθfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>懒惰的，迟缓的</td><td>slothful habits<br>懒散的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of easy living had made the once-diligent clerk slothful.  多年安逸生活使这位曾经勤勉的职员变得懒散。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ineffable</b></td><td>/ɪnˈefəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以表达的</td><td>ineffable joy<br>难以言表的喜悦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pianist played with an ineffable tenderness that silenced the hall.  钢琴家弹出难以言表的温柔，让全场安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indefinable</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈfaɪnəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以定义的，说不清的</td><td>an indefinable charm<br>难以言明的魅力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was an indefinable sadness in the old man's voice.  老人的声音里有一种说不清的悲伤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indescribable</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈskraɪbəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以描述的</td><td>indescribable beauty<br>难以形容的美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The view from the summit at sunrise was simply indescribable.  日出时从峰顶望去的景色简直无法形容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inexpressible</td><td>/ˌɪnɪkˈspresəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法表达的</td><td>inexpressible grief<br>无法言说的悲痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt an inexpressible relief when the letter finally arrived.  信终于来时，她感到难以言表的宽慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unspeakable</td><td>/ʌnˈspiːkəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>说不出的；极坏的</td><td>unspeakable horror<br>难以言说的恐怖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The refugees described unspeakable suffering during the long winter march.  难民们讲述了漫长冬季行军中难以言说的苦难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unutterable</td><td>/ʌnˈʌtərəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法说出的，极度的</td><td>unutterable sorrow<br>无法言说的悲伤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stared at the ruins with unutterable dismay.  他带着无法言说的沮丧凝视着废墟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 communicable</td><td>/kəmˈjuːnəkəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可传达的；传染的</td><td>a communicable disease<br>传染性疾病</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some feelings are barely communicable through words alone.  有些感受几乎无法仅用言语传达。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ineluctable</b></td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈlʌktəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法逃避的，必然的</td><td>an ineluctable conclusion<br>无法回避的结论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Given the evidence, the jury reached the ineluctable conclusion that he was guilty.  鉴于这些证据，陪审团得出了他有罪这一无法回避的结论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 certain</td><td>/ˈsɜːrtən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>确定的，无疑的</td><td>certain of the outcome<br>对结果有把握</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We are certain that the letter was never sent.  我们确信那封信从未寄出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inescapable</td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈskeɪpəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可避免的</td><td>an inescapable conclusion<br>无法逃避的结论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The evidence led to one inescapable conclusion about the fire.  证据指向关于这场火灾的唯一无法回避的结论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inevasible</del></td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈveɪsəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法回避的</td><td>an inevasible duty<br>无法推卸的责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Death is the one inevasible fact that unites all living things.  死亡是联结一切生命的唯一无法回避的事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inevitable</td><td>/ɪnˈevɪtəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可避免的，必然的</td><td>an inevitable outcome<br>必然的结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Given the drought, a poor harvest was inevitable that year.  鉴于旱情，那年歉收在所难免。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unavoidable</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈvɔɪdəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可避免的</td><td>an unavoidable delay<br>无法避免的延误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy fog made the cancellation of the flight unavoidable.  浓雾使航班取消无法避免。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 avoidable</td><td>/əˈvɔɪdəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可避免的</td><td>an avoidable mistake<br>可以避免的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of these accidents were entirely avoidable with proper training.  只要培训得当，这些事故大多完全可以避免。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 evadable</del></td><td>/ɪˈveɪdəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可逃避的，可回避的</td><td>an evadable obligation<br>可以规避的义务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer argued that the obligation was easily evadable under the old contract.  律师主张这项义务在旧合同下很容易规避。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>inept</b></td><td>/ɪˈnept/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚笨的，荒谬的；不称职的，无能力的</td><td>politically inept<br>政治上无能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager was so inept at handling complaints that customers left in droves.  这位经理处理投诉如此无能，导致顾客大量流失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 absurd</td><td>/əbˈsɜːrd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合理的，荒谬的</td><td>an absurd suggestion<br>荒谬的建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It's absurd to expect results in a single week.  期望一周内出结果是荒谬的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foolish</td><td>/ˈfuːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a foolish mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was foolish of him to ignore the warning signs.  他忽视警示标志实在愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fatuous</td><td>/ˈfætʃuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚笨的，昏庸的</td><td>a fatuous remark<br>愚蠢的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fatuous comment showed he had not read the report.  他愚蠢的评论说明他没读过那份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preposterous</td><td>/prɪˈpɑːstərəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>荒谬的，极其反常的</td><td>a preposterous claim<br>荒唐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The idea that he acted alone is preposterous.  说他独自行动的说法荒谬至极。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stupid</td><td>/ˈstuːpəd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，笨的</td><td>a stupid mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I felt stupid after mixing up their names again.  又把他们的名字弄混后我觉得自己很笨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 witless</td><td>/ˈwɪtləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，无头脑的</td><td>a witless remark<br>愚蠢的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witless prank got both boys suspended from school.  这个愚蠢的恶作剧让两个男孩都被停学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unwise</td><td>/ʌnˈwaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，愚蠢的</td><td>an unwise decision<br>不明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Borrowing more money now would be deeply unwise.  现在再借钱是很不明智的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incapable</td><td>/ˌɪnˈkeɪpəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无能力的，不会的</td><td>incapable of lying<br>不会说谎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He seemed incapable of finishing even the simplest task.  他似乎连最简单的任务都无法完成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inexpert</td><td>/ˌɪnˈekspɜːrt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟练的，外行的</td><td>inexpert hands<br>不熟练的手法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 To inexpert eyes the two paintings looked exactly the same.  在外行眼里，这两幅画看起来完全一样。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfitted</td><td>/ʌnˈfɪtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适合的，不胜任的</td><td>unfitted for command<br>不适合指挥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His hot temper left him unfitted for diplomatic work.  他的急躁性格使他不适合外交工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unskillful</td><td>/ʌnˈskɪlfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟练的，技艺拙劣的</td><td>unskillful workmanship<br>拙劣的手工活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unskillful repair left the table wobbling badly.  这次拙劣的修理让桌子摇晃得厉害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unqualified</td><td>/ʌnˈkwɑːlɪfaɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>绝对的，无保留的；不合格的</td><td>unqualified support<br>毫无保留的支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan won her unqualified support at the very first meeting.  这个计划在第一次会上就赢得她毫无保留的支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 judicious</td><td>/dʒuˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，慎重的</td><td>a judicious choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A judicious use of savings carried the family through two difficult years.  明智地使用积蓄让这家人度过了两个艰难的年头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sagacious</td><td>/səˈɡeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>睿智的，有远见的</td><td>a sagacious adviser<br>睿智的顾问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sagacious advice saved the family business from certain ruin.  他睿智的建议救家族生意于必败之境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sapient</td><td>/ˈseɪpiənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，有洞察力的</td><td>a sapient observation<br>有洞察力的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few sapient remarks from the editor saved the entire chapter.  编辑几句有洞察力的话救了整整一章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smart</td><td>/ˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的；时髦的</td><td>a smart move<br>明智之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying the tickets early was a very smart decision.  提前买票是个非常明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wise</td><td>/ˈwaɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，有智慧的</td><td>a wise decision<br>明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was wise of her to save money before quitting the job.  她辞职前先存钱，很明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 capable</td><td>/ˈkeɪpəbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有能力的，能干的</td><td>a capable administrator<br>有能力的管理者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is perfectly capable of running the department alone.  她完全有能力独自管理这个部门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 competent</td><td>/ˈkɑːmpətɪnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有能力的，胜任的</td><td>a competent surgeon<br>称职的外科医生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report was written by a competent but uninspired analyst.  报告出自一位称职却缺乏灵气的分析师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>inert</b></td><td>/ɪˈnɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（人）懒惰缺乏活力的；（物品）惰性的</td><td>an inert gas<br>惰性气体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Helium is an inert gas, so it rarely reacts with other elements.  氦是一种惰性气体，因此很少与其他元素发生反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dead</td><td>/ˈded/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>死的；毫无生气的</td><td>a dead town at night<br>夜里死寂的小镇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The resort is completely dead in the winter months.  这个度假地在冬季几个月里毫无生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dormant</td><td>/ˈdɔːrmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>静止的，不活跃的</td><td>a dormant volcano<br>休眠火山</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The virus can stay dormant in the body for years.  这种病毒可在体内潜伏多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lethargic</td><td>/ləˈθɑːrdʒɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没精打采的，行动迟缓的</td><td>feel lethargic all day<br>整天没精打采</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heat left everyone lethargic and unwilling to move before evening.  酷热让所有人都没精打采，天黑前都不愿动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 idle</td><td>/ˈaɪdl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>闲置的，未使用的；懒散的</td><td>idle machines<br>闲置的机器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Half the factory's machines stood idle for most of last year.  这家工厂一半的机器去年大部分时间都闲置着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inactive</td><td>/ɪnˈæktɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不活动的，不活跃的</td><td>remain inactive for months<br>数月毫无动作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The volcano has been inactive for over two hundred years.  这座火山已有两百多年没有活动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 passive</td><td>/ˈpæsɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>被动的，消极的</td><td>a passive observer<br>被动的旁观者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed passive while others argued fiercely about the plan.  别人激烈争论方案时，他始终无所作为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 torpid</td><td>/ˈtɔːrpɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的，无生气的</td><td>a torpid winter afternoon<br>慵懒迟滞的冬日下午</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heat left the whole village torpid and unwilling to move.  酷热让整个村子都变得麻木迟钝，不愿动弹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 active</td><td>/ˈæktɪv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>活跃的，活动的</td><td>an active volcano<br>活火山</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stays active by swimming every morning.  她每天早上游泳以保持活跃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dynamic</td><td>/daɪˈnæmɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>有活力的，动态的</td><td>a dynamic leader<br>有活力的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city has a dynamic arts scene that keeps changing.  这座城市有着不断变化的活跃艺术圈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>inevitable</b></td><td>/ɪnˈevɪtəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可避免的，必然的</td><td>an inevitable outcome<br>必然的结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Given the drought, a poor harvest was inevitable that year.  鉴于旱情，那年歉收在所难免。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 certain</td><td>/ˈsɜːrtən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>确定的，无疑的</td><td>certain of the outcome<br>对结果有把握</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We are certain that the letter was never sent.  我们确信那封信从未寄出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ineluctable</td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈlʌktəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法逃避的，必然的</td><td>an ineluctable conclusion<br>无法回避的结论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Given the evidence, the jury reached the ineluctable conclusion that he was guilty.  鉴于这些证据，陪审团得出了他有罪这一无法回避的结论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inescapable</td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈskeɪpəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可避免的</td><td>an inescapable conclusion<br>无法逃避的结论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The evidence led to one inescapable conclusion about the fire.  证据指向关于这场火灾的唯一无法回避的结论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inevasible</del></td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈveɪsəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法回避的</td><td>an inevasible duty<br>无法推卸的责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Death is the one inevasible fact that unites all living things.  死亡是联结一切生命的唯一无法回避的事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sure</td><td>/ˈʃʊr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>确定的，可靠的</td><td>a sure sign<br>可靠的迹象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Falling leaves are a sure sign that autumn has arrived.  落叶是秋天已到的可靠迹象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unalterable</td><td>/ʌnˈɔːltərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可改变的</td><td>an unalterable decision<br>不可更改的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The terms of the contract are unalterable once both parties sign.  合同条款一经双方签署便不可更改。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unavoidable</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈvɔɪdəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可避免的</td><td>an unavoidable delay<br>无法避免的延误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy fog made the cancellation of the flight unavoidable.  浓雾使航班取消无法避免。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 evitable</del></td><td>/ˈevɪtəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可避免的</td><td>an evitable error<br>可以避免的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The engineer insisted the collapse had been entirely evitable.  工程师坚称这次倒塌本来完全可以避免。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 avoidable</td><td>/əˈvɔɪdəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可避免的</td><td>an avoidable mistake<br>可以避免的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of these accidents were entirely avoidable with proper training.  只要培训得当，这些事故大多完全可以避免。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>inexorable</b></td><td>/ɪnˈeksərəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法劝阻的，不为所动的</td><td>the inexorable march of time<br>时间无情的推进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The inexorable rise of the tide slowly swallowed the sandcastle we built.  潮水无可阻挡地上涨，慢慢吞没了我们堆的沙堡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inflexible</td><td>/ˌɪnˈfleksəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不肯变通的，僵化的</td><td>an inflexible rule<br>不容变通的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager was inflexible about the submission deadline.  经理在提交截止日期上毫不通融。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adamant</td><td>/ˈædəmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，不可动摇的</td><td>adamant about the rules<br>对规则毫不通融</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach was adamant that no player would skip practice.  教练坚决要求没有球员可以缺席训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obdurate</td><td>/ˈɑːbdərət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的；冷酷无情的</td><td>remain obdurate<br>仍然固执不改</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The landlord was obdurate and refused to lower the rent.  房东顽固不化，拒绝降低房租。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relentless</td><td>/rɪˈlentləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>残酷的，无情的；固执的，不肯妥协的</td><td>relentless pressure<br>无休止的压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Relentless pressure from creditors finally forced the small firm to close.  债权人无情的压力最终迫使这家小公司关门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rigid</td><td>/ˈrɪdʒəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严格的，僵硬的</td><td>rigid discipline<br>严格的纪律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school enforces rigid rules about mobile phones.  学校对手机执行严格的规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unyielding</td><td>/ʌnˈjiːldɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不屈的，不让步的</td><td>unyielding opposition<br>毫不让步的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union remained unyielding on the question of wages.  工会在工资问题上依然毫不让步。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flexible</td><td>/ˈfleksəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>灵活的，可变的；易受影响的</td><td>a flexible schedule<br>灵活的日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offers a flexible schedule to parents of young children.  公司为有幼儿的员工提供灵活的日程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>infamous</b></td><td>/ˈɪnfəməs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>臭名昭著的</td><td>an infamous crime<br>臭名昭著的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dictator remains infamous for the brutal purges he ordered during his rule.  这位独裁者因其统治期间下令进行的残酷清洗而臭名昭著。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 notorious</td><td>/noʊˈtɔːriəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>声名狼藉的</td><td>notorious for corruption<br>因腐败而臭名昭著</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neighborhood was notorious for its late-night noise and crime.  这个街区因深夜噪音和犯罪而声名狼藉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 opprobrious</del></td><td>/əˈproʊbriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辱骂的；臭名昭著的，可耻的</td><td>opprobrious remarks<br>辱骂性的言辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet was filled with opprobrious attacks on the mayor.  那本小册子充满了对市长的辱骂性攻击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 distinguished</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卓越的，杰出的；高贵的</td><td>a distinguished scholar<br>杰出的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prize went to a distinguished historian of medieval law.  奖项授予一位杰出的中世纪法律史学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 esteemed</td><td>/ɪˈstiːmd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>受尊敬的</td><td>an esteemed colleague<br>受敬重的同事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The award honored an esteemed scholar of ancient languages.  该奖项表彰了一位受人敬重的古语言学者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prestigious</td><td>/preˈstɪdʒəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有声望的，受尊敬的</td><td>a prestigious award<br>有声望的奖项</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned down a prestigious post to stay near his family.  他为了留在家人身边拒绝了一个有声望的职位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reputable</td><td>/ˈrepjətəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>声誉好的，可信的</td><td>a reputable dealer<br>信誉良好的经销商</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buy the parts from a reputable supplier rather than an auction site.  从信誉良好的供应商买零件，而不是拍卖网站。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>infatuate</b></del></td><td>/ɪnˈfætʃueɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷恋</td><td>be infatuated with someone<br>迷恋某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The naive girl was infatuated with a man she barely knew.  那个天真的女孩迷恋上了一个她几乎不了解的男人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 allure</td><td>/əˈlʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>吸引，诱惑</td><td>the allure of fame<br>名声的诱惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap rents allure young artists to this run-down district.  低廉的租金吸引年轻艺术家来到这个破旧街区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 captivate</td><td>/ˈkæptɪveɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使着迷，深深吸引</td><td>captivate the audience<br>深深吸引观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel captivated readers around the world.  她的第一部小说令世界各地的读者着迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bewitch</td><td>/bɪˈwɪtʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使着迷；用魔法迷住</td><td>bewitch the audience<br>让观众为之着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her singing bewitched everyone in the crowded little hall.  她的歌声让拥挤小厅里的每个人都着了迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enchant</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃænt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使入迷，使陶醉</td><td>be enchanted by the scenery<br>被风景迷住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old fishing village enchanted every visitor who stayed there.  那个古老渔村令每位住过的游客陶醉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fascinate</td><td>/ˈfæsɪneɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈吸引，使入迷</td><td>fascinate young readers<br>使小读者着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stories about strange deep-sea creatures fascinate young readers all over the world.  关于奇异深海生物的故事让全世界的小读者着迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disgust</td><td>/dɪsˈɡʌst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使厌恶；厌恶</td><td>disgust even his allies<br>连他的盟友也感到厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The state of the kitchen disgusted the health inspector.  厨房的状况让卫生检查员十分厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>incommensurate</b></del></td><td>/ˌɪnkəˈmɛnsərɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相称的，不成比例的</td><td>incommensurate with the effort<br>与付出不相称</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tiny reward was incommensurate with the years of work.  这点微薄的报酬与多年的付出不相称。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disproportionate</td><td>/ˌdɪsprəˈpɔːrʃənɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不成比例的，不相称的</td><td>a disproportionate share<br>不成比例的份额</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rural schools receive a disproportionate share of the budget cuts.  农村学校承担了不成比例的预算削减。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>infiltrate</b></td><td>/ˈɪnfɪltreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>秘密潜入</td><td>infiltrate enemy lines<br>渗透进敌方阵线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two agents managed to infiltrate the organization and report on its secret plans.  两名特工成功潜入该组织并报告了它的秘密计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 creep</td><td>/kriːp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>缓慢地行进</td><td>creep along slowly<br>缓慢地爬行前进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic crept forward only a few meters at a time all morning.  整个上午车流每次只缓慢前进几米。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insinuate</td><td>/ɪnˈsɪnjueɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>含沙射影地暗示；使巧妙混入</td><td>insinuate that someone lied<br>暗指某人说了谎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He insinuated that the accounts had been quietly altered.  他暗示账目被人悄悄改动过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 penetrate</td><td>/ˈpenəˌtreɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>穿透，渗入</td><td>penetrate the market<br>打入市场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sunlight barely penetrates the dense canopy of the rainforest.  阳光几乎无法穿透雨林茂密的树冠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sneak</td><td>/ˈsniːk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>偷偷地走，溜</td><td>sneak into the room<br>偷偷溜进房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children sneaked out of the house after midnight.  孩子们午夜后偷偷溜出了房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slip</td><td>/ˈslɪp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>溜走；滑倒</td><td>slip through the crowd<br>从人群中溜过</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She slipped quietly out of the meeting before the vote.  她在投票前悄悄溜出了会议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abscond</td><td>/əbˈskɑːnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>偷偷离开，潜逃</td><td>abscond with the funds<br>携款潜逃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treasurer absconded with thousands of dollars.  财务主管带着数千美元潜逃了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>infinite</b></td><td>/ˈɪnfɪnət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无尽的，无限的</td><td>infinite patience<br>无穷的耐心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teaching young children requires infinite patience and a genuine sense of humor.  教年幼的孩子需要无穷的耐心和真正的幽默感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 endless</td><td>/ˈendləs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无尽的，无休止的</td><td>endless complaints<br>无休止的抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The meeting felt endless, though it lasted only ninety minutes.  会议感觉没完没了，其实只开了九十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boundless</td><td>/ˈbaʊndləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无限的，无边的</td><td>boundless enthusiasm<br>无限的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child greeted every new day with boundless energy.  这孩子以无穷的精力迎接每一天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 limitless</td><td>/ˈlɪmətləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无限的</td><td>limitless possibilities<br>无限的可能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The internet offers seemingly limitless sources of information.  互联网提供看似无限的信息来源。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immeasurable</td><td>/ɪˈmeʒərəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可估量的</td><td>immeasurable harm<br>无法估量的伤害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The war caused immeasurable damage to the country's cultural heritage.  战争对该国文化遗产造成了不可估量的损害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfathomable</td><td>/ənˈfæðəməbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的，深不可测的</td><td>an unfathomable mystery<br>难以理解的谜团</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His motives remained unfathomable even to his closest friends.  连最亲近的朋友也无法理解他的动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 finite</td><td>/ˈfaɪˌnaɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有限的</td><td>finite resources<br>有限的资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We must plan carefully because our budget is finite.  我们必须谨慎规划，因为预算是有限的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>infirm</b></td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的；不果断的，优柔寡断的</td><td>old and infirm<br>年老体弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her grandmother grew too infirm to climb the stairs without help.  她的祖母身体太虚弱，没人帮忙就爬不了楼梯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debilitated</td><td>/dəˈbɪləˌteɪtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的，衰弱的</td><td>debilitated by long illness<br>因久病而衰弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The patient looked pale and debilitated after months of treatment.  经过数月治疗，病人显得苍白虚弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 effete</td><td>/ɪˈfiːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>衰弱的，衰落的</td><td>an effete aristocracy<br>衰落的贵族阶层</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An effete aristocracy clung to its titles long after the wealth had vanished.  衰落的贵族阶层在财富早已消失后仍死守头衔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enervated</td><td>/ˈɛnərveɪtəd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的，无力的</td><td>enervated by the heat<br>被暑热弄得虚弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long illness left him enervated and unusually pale.  长期患病使他虚弱且异常苍白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 feeble</td><td>/ˈfiːbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>衰弱的</td><td>a feeble voice<br>微弱的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man answered in a feeble voice from the bed.  老人从床上用微弱的声音回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frail</td><td>/freɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的；脆弱的</td><td>a frail old woman<br>体弱的老妇人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A frail old woman waited alone at the crowded bus stop.  一位虚弱的老妇人独自等在拥挤的公交站。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 languid</td><td>/ˈlæŋɡwɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没精打采的，虚弱的</td><td>a languid summer afternoon<br>慢悠悠的夏日午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A languid summer afternoon passed with nobody willing to leave the shade.  一个慢悠悠的夏日午后过去了，没人愿意离开树荫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sapped</td><td>/ˈsæpt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>力气被耗尽的，虚弱的</td><td>sapped of energy<br>精力被耗尽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team looked sapped after three games in one week.  一周三场比赛后球队看来精力耗尽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsubstantial</td><td>/ˌʌnsəbˈstænʃəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不结实的；空洞无实质的</td><td>unsubstantial evidence<br>缺乏实质的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The case rested on thin, unsubstantial evidence.  这个案子建立在单薄而缺乏实质的证据上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 faltering</td><td>/ˈfɔːltərɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>犹豫的；蹒跚的</td><td>a faltering voice<br>犹豫颤抖的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered in a faltering voice, unsure of herself.  她声音犹豫地回答，显得没有把握。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 irresolute</del></td><td>/ɪˈrezəluːt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>犹豫不决的</td><td>an irresolute leader<br>优柔寡断的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The irresolute committee postponed the decision for another month.  犹豫不决的委员会又把决定推迟了一个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vacillating</td><td>/ˈvæsəˌleɪtɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>摇摆不定的，犹豫的</td><td>a vacillating leader<br>摇摆不定的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company suffered under a vacillating chief executive.  公司在一位摇摆不定的总裁手下吃尽苦头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wavering</td><td>/ˈweɪvərɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>动摇的，游移不定的</td><td>wavering support<br>动摇的支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Polls showed the wavering support of rural voters.  民调显示农村选民的支持正在动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hale</td><td>/heɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强壮的，健壮的</td><td>hale and hearty<br>身体强健，精神饱满</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At eighty he remains hale enough to walk five miles a day.  八十岁的他依然强健，每天能走五英里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mighty</td><td>/ˈmaɪti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强大的，有力的</td><td>a mighty river<br>浩荡的大河</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A mighty oak had stood there for three centuries.  一棵巨大的橡树在那里矗立了三个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 powerful</td><td>/ˈpaʊərfəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强大的，有力的</td><td>a powerful engine<br>强劲的发动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The telescope is powerful enough to show distant galaxies.  这台望远镜足够强大，能显示遥远的星系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rugged</td><td>/ˈrʌɡəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚毅的；粗糙不平的</td><td>rugged terrain<br>险峻的地形</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only rugged travelers attempt that trail in the winter.  只有意志坚毅的旅行者才会在冬天走那条小径。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stalwart</td><td>/ˈstɔːlwərt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定忠诚的；强壮的</td><td>a stalwart supporter<br>坚定的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remained a stalwart supporter of the project through every setback.  历经挫折，她始终是这个项目的坚定支持者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stout</td><td>/ˈstaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的，勇敢的；粗壮的</td><td>stout resistance<br>顽强的抵抗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The townspeople put up a stout defense against the raiders.  镇民对袭击者进行了顽强的抵抗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 strong</td><td>/strɔːŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强壮的，坚固的</td><td>strong arms<br>强壮的臂膀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge is strong enough to carry heavy trucks.  这座桥足够坚固，能承载重型卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 resolute</td><td>/ˈrezəluːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的</td><td>remain resolute<br>保持坚定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite heavy criticism, the mayor remained resolute about closing the old factory.  尽管备受批评，市长在关闭旧工厂一事上依然坚定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>inflame</b></td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧</td><td>inflame public anger<br>使公众愤怒加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's careless comments only inflamed public anger over the housing shortage.  市长轻率的言论只是加剧了公众对住房短缺的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enrage</td><td>/enˈreɪdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使愤怒，激怒</td><td>enrage the audience<br>激怒观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His flippant apology only enraged the families further.  他轻率的道歉只让那些家庭更加愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exacerbate</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæsərbeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧，使恶化</td><td>exacerbate the crisis<br>使危机加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting the budget would only exacerbate the housing shortage.  削减预算只会使住房短缺进一步恶化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intensify</td><td>/ɪnˈtensɪfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>加强，激化</td><td>intensify the pressure<br>加大压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain intensified just as we reached the summit.  我们刚到山顶，雨就更大了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ire</td><td>/ˈaɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>愤怒，怒火</td><td>draw the ire of critics<br>引来批评者的怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax drew the ire of small business owners.  新税收招致了小企业主的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irritate</td><td>/ˈɪrɪteɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒；刺激</td><td>irritate the customers<br>使顾客恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud advertisements before every video irritate most of our customers.  每个视频前的大声广告让我们大多数客户感到恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 provoke</td><td>/prəˈvoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒；驱使，激起</td><td>provoke an angry response<br>引发愤怒的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless remark provoked an angry response from the whole audience.  他不慎的言论激起了全体观众的愤怒回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rile</td><td>/raɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，惹怒</td><td>rile up the crowd<br>激怒人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing riles him more than being interrupted in the middle of a sentence.  没有什么比话说到一半被打断更让他恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vex</td><td>/veks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使恼怒</td><td>vex the teacher<br>使老师恼怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant interruptions vexed the teacher throughout the long afternoon lesson.  不断的打扰让老师在漫长的下午课上十分恼怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mitigate</td><td>/ˈmɪtɪɡeɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，使缓和</td><td>mitigate the damage<br>减轻损害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Planting trees can mitigate the effects of summer heat.  种树可以减轻夏季高温的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacify</td><td>/ˈpæsɪfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使平静，安慰</td><td>pacify a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse sang softly to pacify the frightened child before the injection.  打针前，护士轻声唱歌安抚受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 placate</td><td>/ˈpleɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过让步以）平息抚慰</td><td>placate the critics<br>平息批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered full refunds to placate its angriest customers.  公司提供全额退款以平息最愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 subdue</td><td>/səbˈduː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使顺从；征服</td><td>subdue the rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Government forces moved south to subdue the rebellion within a week.  政府军南下，一周内镇压了叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>infelicitous</b></del></td><td>/ɪnfɪˈlɪsɪtəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不愉快的，不幸的；不适当的，不合时宜的</td><td>an infelicitous remark<br>不合时宜的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His infelicitous remark about her family ruined the otherwise pleasant dinner party.  他关于她家庭的不合时宜的话毁了本来愉快的晚宴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hapless</td><td>/ˈhæpləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倒霉的，不幸的</td><td>the hapless victim<br>倒霉的受害者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hapless traveler lost both his passport and wallet.  这位倒霉的旅客丢了护照和钱包。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ill-fated</td><td>/ˌɪl ˈfeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>注定不幸的，命运多灾的</td><td>an ill-fated expedition<br>注定不幸的远征</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only two men returned from that ill-fated expedition.  那次注定不幸的远征只有两人生还。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luckless</td><td>/ˈlʌkləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不幸的，倒霉的</td><td>a luckless gambler<br>倒霉的赌徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The luckless team lost every match of the season.  这支倒霉的球队本赛季场场皆输。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfortunate</td><td>/ʌnˈfɔːrtʃənət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不幸的，遗憾的</td><td>an unfortunate mistake<br>不幸的失误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was unfortunate that the letter arrived a week too late.  遗憾的是那封信迟到了一周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unhappy</td><td>/ʌnˈhæpi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不快乐的；不恰当的</td><td>an unhappy childhood<br>不幸的童年</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was deeply unhappy during his first year abroad.  他在国外的第一年非常不开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inappropriate</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈproʊpriət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适当的，不合适的</td><td>inappropriate behavior at work<br>工作中的不当行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing shorts to the interview was clearly inappropriate.  穿短裤去面试显然不合适。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indecorous</td><td>/ɪnˈdekərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合乎礼节的，不得体的</td><td>indecorous remarks at the ceremony<br>典礼上不得体的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His indecorous jokes at the memorial service embarrassed nearly everyone in the room.  他在追悼会上不得体的玩笑让在场几乎所有人都很尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inept</td><td>/ɪˈnept/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚笨的，荒谬的；不称职的，无能力的</td><td>politically inept<br>政治上无能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager was so inept at handling complaints that customers left in droves.  这位经理处理投诉如此无能，导致顾客大量流失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 malapropos</del></td><td>/ˌmæləprəˈpoʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>不合时宜的</td><td>a malapropos joke<br>不合时宜的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His malapropos comment about money embarrassed everyone at dinner.  他关于钱的不合时宜的评论让餐桌上所有人尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfit</td><td>/ʌnˈfɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适合的；不健康的</td><td>unfit for human consumption<br>不适合人类食用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old bridge was declared unfit for heavy traffic.  这座老桥被判定不适合重型车辆通行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unseemly</td><td>/ʌnˈsiːmli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不得体的，不适宜的</td><td>unseemly haste<br>失礼的匆忙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their unseemly quarrel spoiled the whole wedding reception.  他们不得体的争吵毁了整场婚宴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lucky</td><td>/ˈlʌki/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>幸运的</td><td>a lucky escape<br>幸运的脱险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They had a lucky escape when the roof collapsed.  屋顶塌落时他们幸运地脱险了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 happy</td><td>/ˈhæpi/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快乐的，幸福的</td><td>a happy ending<br>幸福的结局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children were happy to see snow for the first time.  孩子们第一次见到雪很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apt</td><td>/æpt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恰当的，合适的；聪明的</td><td>an apt description<br>恰当的描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That is an apt description of how the meeting actually went.  那是对会议实际情况的一个恰当描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appropriate</td><td>/əˈproʊprieɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>私自挪用；适当的</td><td>appropriate public funds<br>挪用公款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials were accused of appropriating public money for private use.  官员们被指控将公款挪为私用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 becoming</td><td>/bɪˈkʌmɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的，得体的</td><td>a becoming dress<br>得体的裙子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her modest reply was becoming in such a formal setting.  在如此正式的场合，她谦逊的回答很得体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 proper</td><td>/ˈprɑːpər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>适当的，正确的</td><td>the proper procedure<br>正确的程序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please store the samples in the proper containers.  请把样本存放在合适的容器里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>influx</b></td><td>/ˈɪnflʌks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>涌入</td><td>an influx of tourists<br>游客涌入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden influx of tourists overwhelmed the small town's hotels and restaurants.  游客突然涌入，使这个小镇的旅馆和餐馆不堪重负。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affluence</td><td>/ˈæfluəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>富裕，丰富</td><td>live in affluence<br>生活富裕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family had lived in quiet affluence for three generations.  这家人已在平静的富裕中生活了三代。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flux</td><td>/ˈflʌks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>流动，涌流；不断变化</td><td>in a state of flux<br>处于不断变化中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole industry is in flux as new regulations take effect.  新规生效后整个行业都在剧烈变动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 income</td><td>/ˈɪnˌkʌm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>收入，进项</td><td>a steady monthly income<br>稳定的月收入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their household income barely covers rent and groceries.  他们的家庭收入勉强够付房租和食品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inflow</td><td>/ˈɪnˌfloʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>流入，涌入</td><td>an inflow of capital<br>资本流入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sudden inflow of foreign money pushed local housing prices higher.  外资突然涌入推高了当地房价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inpouring</del></td><td>/ˈɪnpɔːrɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>涌入，流入</td><td>an inpouring of donations<br>捐款的涌入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity was surprised by the inpouring of public support.  慈善机构对公众支持的涌入感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inrush</td><td>/ˈɪnrʌʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>涌入，闯入</td><td>an inrush of cold air<br>冷空气涌入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An inrush of cold air followed him through the heavy door.  一股冷空气随他穿过厚重的门涌入。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exodus</td><td>/ˈeksədəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大批离去</td><td>a mass exodus<br>大批离去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory closure caused a mass exodus from the small town.  工厂关闭造成大批人离开这个小镇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>infuriate</b></td><td>/ɪnˈfjʊrieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>infuriate the critics<br>激怒批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision to cut pensions infuriated thousands of workers across the country.  削减养老金的决定激怒了全国成千上万的工人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enrage</td><td>/enˈreɪdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使愤怒，激怒</td><td>enrage the audience<br>激怒观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His flippant apology only enraged the families further.  他轻率的道歉只让那些家庭更加愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exasperate</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæspəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，激怒</td><td>exasperate his parents<br>使他的父母恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His constant lateness exasperated even the most patient teachers.  他总是迟到，连最有耐心的老师都被激怒了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incense</td><td>/ɪnˈsens/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>incense the public<br>激怒公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal is certain to incense feminists across the country.  这项提案肯定会激怒全国的女权主义者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ire</td><td>/ˈaɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>愤怒，怒火</td><td>draw the ire of critics<br>引来批评者的怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax drew the ire of small business owners.  新税收招致了小企业主的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 madden</td><td>/ˈmædən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使发狂，使愤怒</td><td>madden the audience<br>使观众暴怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The endless delays maddened passengers waiting at the gate.  无休止的延误让在登机口等待的乘客怒不可遏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appease</td><td>/əˈpiːz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；满足</td><td>appease the angry crowd<br>安抚愤怒的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered refunds to appease its angry customers.  公司提供退款以安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacify</td><td>/ˈpæsɪfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使平静，安慰</td><td>pacify a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse sang softly to pacify the frightened child before the injection.  打针前，护士轻声唱歌安抚受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 placate</td><td>/ˈpleɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过让步以）平息抚慰</td><td>placate the critics<br>平息批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered full refunds to placate its angriest customers.  公司提供全额退款以平息最愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 propitiate</td><td>/prəˈpɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抚慰，劝解</td><td>propitiate the angry gods<br>抚慰愤怒的神灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers offered rice and flowers to propitiate the mountain spirits.  村民献上米和花来抚慰山神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>infuse</b></td><td>/ɪnˈfjuːz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>灌输，使…充满</td><td>infuse new energy into<br>注入新的活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new members infused fresh energy into the struggling drama club.  新成员为陷入困境的戏剧社注入了新的活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 endue</del></td><td>/ɪnˈduː/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赋予（才能、品质）</td><td>endue with courage<br>赋予勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nature had endued him with an extraordinary memory for faces.  自然赋予他辨认面孔的非凡记忆力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imbue</td><td>/ɪmˈbjuː/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>灌输，使充满</td><td>imbue students with confidence<br>使学生充满自信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her teachers imbued her with a lasting love of poetry.  老师们使她对诗歌怀有持久的热爱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 implant</td><td>/ˌɪmˈplænt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>植入，灌输</td><td>implant an idea<br>植入一个想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film implanted a fear of deep water in many viewers.  这部电影在许多观众心中植入了对深水的恐惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ingrain</td><td>/ɪnˈɡreɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使根深蒂固，深植</td><td>ingrain a sense of duty<br>把责任感深植于心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of training ingrained in her the habit of checking every figure twice.  多年训练使她养成了把每个数字复查两遍的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 instill</td><td>/ɪnˈstɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>灌输</td><td>instill discipline in children<br>向孩子灌输纪律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good coaches instill discipline in children without crushing their natural curiosity.  好教练向孩子灌输纪律，却不压抑他们天生的好奇心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 permeate</td><td>/ˈpɜːrmieɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>弥漫，渗透</td><td>permeate the whole building<br>弥漫整栋楼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell of fresh bread permeated the entire building by seven.  七点前，新鲜面包的香味弥漫了整栋楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 suffuse</del></td><td>/səˈfjuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（色彩等）弥漫，染遍，充满</td><td>suffused with warm light<br>沐浴在暖光中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm evening light suffused the quiet room with a golden glow.  傍晚的暖光把安静的房间染成金色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 steep</td><td>/stiːp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>浸泡，浸渍</td><td>steep tea leaves in hot water<br>把茶叶泡在热水里</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Steep the dried leaves in boiling water for about ten minutes.  把干叶子在沸水里浸泡约十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extract</td><td>/ɪkˈstrækt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拔出，抽出；提取</td><td>extract a bad tooth<br>拔掉一颗坏牙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dentist had to extract a badly decayed back tooth.  牙医不得不拔掉一颗严重腐坏的后牙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ingenious</b></td><td>/ɪnˈdʒiːniəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，有创造才能的</td><td>an ingenious solution<br>巧妙的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She devised an ingenious method for storing rainwater during the dry season.  她想出了一个在旱季储存雨水的巧妙方法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 artful</td><td>/ˈɑːrtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>技艺高超的；狡猾的</td><td>an artful arrangement<br>巧妙的布置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chef's artful plating made the dish look like a painting.  厨师巧妙的摆盘让这道菜看起来像幅画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clever</td><td>/ˈklevər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，机敏的</td><td>a clever solution<br>巧妙的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That was a clever way to avoid the extra cost.  那是避免额外成本的聪明办法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imaginative</td><td>/ɪˈmædʒənətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>富有想象力的</td><td>an imaginative writer<br>富有想象力的作家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum's imaginative displays delighted both children and adults.  博物馆富有想象力的展品让儿童和成人都很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 innovative</td><td>/ˈɪnəveɪtɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新性的</td><td>innovative design<br>创新的设计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The firm survived the crisis thanks to an innovative approach to distribution.  这家公司靠创新的分销方式度过了危机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inventive</td><td>/ɪnˈventɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有创造力的，善发明的</td><td>an inventive chef<br>有创意的厨师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers found an inventive way to recycle factory waste heat.  工程师找到了回收工厂余热的创新办法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 original</td><td>/əˈrɪdʒənl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新的；最初的，最早的</td><td>an original idea<br>有创意的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her proposal was genuinely original, unlike the tired ideas we usually hear.  她的提案确实新颖，不像我们常听到的那些陈旧想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 awkward</td><td>/ˈɔːkwərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，不灵巧的；尴尬的</td><td>an awkward silence<br>尴尬的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An awkward silence followed his remark about her divorce.  他谈到她离婚之后，出现了一阵尴尬的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>intermediary</b></td><td>/ˌɪntərˈmiːdieri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>中间人</td><td>act as an intermediary<br>充当中间人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A neutral diplomat acted as intermediary between the government and the rebels.  一位中立的外交官充当了政府与反叛者之间的中间人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 broker</td><td>/ˈbroʊkər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>经纪人；中间人</td><td>an insurance broker<br>保险经纪人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A neutral broker helped the two sides reach an agreement.  一位中立的中间人帮助双方达成协议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conciliator</td><td>/kənˈsɪlieɪtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>调解人，调停者</td><td>an impartial conciliator<br>公正的调解人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union asked a professional conciliator to join the talks.  工会请了一位专业调解人参与谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intercessor</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈsesər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>调停者</td><td>serve as an intercessor<br>充当调停者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A respected elder served as intercessor between the two feuding families.  一位受人尊敬的长者充当了两个敌对家族之间的调停者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mediator</td><td>/ˈmiːdieɪtər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>调解人，中间人</td><td>a neutral mediator<br>中立的调解人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A court-appointed mediator settled the long dispute between neighbors.  法院指定的调解人解决了邻里间的长期纠纷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ingenuous</b></td><td>/ɪnˈdʒenjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天真淳朴的</td><td>an ingenuous young student<br>天真淳朴的年轻学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ingenuous questions about the city amused the veteran taxi driver greatly.  他关于这座城市的天真提问让老出租车司机大为好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 artless</td><td>/ˈɑrtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏技巧的，笨拙的</td><td>an artless imitation<br>拙劣的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting was an artless copy of a famous portrait.  这幅画是对一幅名作的拙劣临摹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 innocent</td><td>/ˈɪnəsənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，无恶意的；无辜的</td><td>an innocent remark<br>无恶意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 What began as an innocent joke ended in a serious quarrel.  一句本无恶意的玩笑最后引发了激烈争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 guileless</td><td>/ˈɡaɪlləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单纯的，不狡诈的</td><td>a guileless smile<br>天真无邪的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her guileless smile made everyone in the room trust her immediately.  她天真无邪的笑容让屋里所有人立刻信任她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 naive</td><td>/naɪˈiːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天真的，幼稚的</td><td>naive assumptions<br>天真的假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was naive to expect honesty from someone with that record.  指望有那种记录的人诚实太天真了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 simple</td><td>/ˈsɪmpəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单的，容易的</td><td>a simple task<br>简单的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Follow these simple steps and the machine will restart.  按这些简单步骤操作，机器就会重启。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unaffected</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈfektɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不受影响的</td><td>unaffected by the crisis<br>未受危机影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Northern farms remained largely unaffected by the drought.  北方的农场基本未受这场旱灾影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unpretending</del></td><td>/ˌʌnprɪˈtendɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不做作的，朴实的</td><td>an unpretending manner<br>朴实无华的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite his fame, he kept an unpretending and quiet lifestyle.  尽管成名，他仍保持朴实安静的生活方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsophisticated</td><td>/ˌʌnsəˈfɪstɪkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单纯的，不老练的</td><td>an unsophisticated buyer<br>不老练的买家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unsophisticated investors are often the first to lose their money.  不老练的投资者往往最先赔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 artful</td><td>/ˈɑːrtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>技艺高超的；狡猾的</td><td>an artful arrangement<br>巧妙的布置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chef's artful plating made the dish look like a painting.  厨师巧妙的摆盘让这道菜看起来像幅画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cunning</td><td>/ˈkʌnɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>a cunning scheme<br>狡猾的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fox was cunning enough to avoid every trap they set.  这只狐狸够狡猾，避开了他们设的每个陷阱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sly</td><td>/slaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>a sly grin<br>狡黠的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sly grin spread across his face as he hid the letter.  他藏起那封信时，脸上露出狡黠的笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ingest</b></td><td>/ɪnˈdʒest/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>摄入，咽下</td><td>ingest contaminated water<br>摄入受污染的水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers who ingest contaminated water often develop serious stomach illnesses.  摄入受污染水源的村民常患严重的胃病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eat</td><td>/ˈiːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>吃，进食</td><td>eat a light breakfast<br>吃份清淡早餐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We usually eat dinner together at seven in the evening.  我们通常晚上七点一起吃晚饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 consume</td><td>/kənˈsuːm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>消耗，耗尽</td><td>consume vast amounts of energy<br>消耗大量能源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Data centers consume vast amounts of electricity every single day.  数据中心每天消耗大量电力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devour</td><td>/dɪˈvaʊər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>吞食，耗尽</td><td>devour the entire meal<br>吞掉整顿饭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hungry children devoured the entire meal in five minutes.  饥饿的孩子们五分钟内吞掉了整顿饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intake</td><td>/ˈɪnˌteɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>摄入量；吸入</td><td>reduce salt intake<br>减少盐的摄入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors advised him to cut his daily calorie intake.  医生建议他减少每日热量摄入。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 evacuate</td><td>/ɪˈvækjueɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>撤离</td><td>evacuate the building<br>撤离大楼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters evacuated the entire building before the roof collapsed.  消防员在屋顶塌落前撤离了整栋大楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expel</td><td>/ɪkˈspel/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>驱逐；开除</td><td>expel a student<br>开除一名学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school expelled two students for cheating on the exam.  学校因考试作弊开除了两名学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ingrained</b></td><td>/ɪnˈɡreɪnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>本质的，根深蒂固的</td><td>deeply ingrained habits<br>根深蒂固的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Habits ingrained since childhood are extremely difficult to change in later life.  童年就已根深蒂固的习惯到了晚年极难改变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 constitutional</td><td>/ˌkɑːnstəˈtuːʃənəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>体质上的，内在固有的</td><td>a constitutional weakness<br>体质上的虚弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His shyness seems constitutional rather than a habit he learned.  他的害羞似乎是天性使然，而非后天习得的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immanent</td><td>/ˈɪmənənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>内在的</td><td>immanent in nature<br>内在于自然之中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He argued that order is immanent in nature rather than imposed on it.  他认为秩序内在于自然，而非外加于自然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inborn</td><td>/ˈɪnˌbɔːrn/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>天生的，先天的</td><td>an inborn talent<br>天生的才能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She seems to have an inborn sense of rhythm.  她似乎天生就有节奏感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indigenous</td><td>/ɪnˈdɪdʒənəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>土产的，本地的；与生俱来的，先天的</td><td>indigenous plants of the region<br>该地区的本土植物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many indigenous species of the island cannot survive anywhere else on the planet.  这个岛上许多本土物种无法在地球其他任何地方存活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inherent</td><td>/ɪnˈhɪrənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>内在的，本质的</td><td>an inherent risk<br>固有的风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is an inherent danger in climbing without a rope.  无绳攀岩存在内在的危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 innate</td><td>/ɪˈneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>天生的，固有的</td><td>an innate ability<br>天生的能力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children have an innate curiosity about the world.  儿童对世界有一种天生的好奇心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intrinsic</td><td>/ɪnˈtrɪnsɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固有的，内在的</td><td>intrinsic value<br>内在价值</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting has an intrinsic beauty independent of its price.  这幅画有着与价格无关的内在美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adventitious</td><td>/ˌædvenˈtɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>外来的，后天的，非内在的</td><td>an adventitious circumstance<br>偶然的外部情况</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her success was not adventitious but the result of years of work.  她的成功不是偶然外来的，而是多年努力的结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ingratiating</b></td><td>/ɪnˈɡreɪʃieɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>讨人喜欢的；逢迎的，意在奉承的</td><td>an ingratiating smile<br>讨好的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salesman's ingratiating manner made me suspect he was hiding something important.  这位销售员逢迎的态度让我怀疑他隐瞒了重要的事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disarming</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːrmɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>消除敌意的，讨喜的</td><td>a disarming smile<br>令人释怀的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His disarming honesty won over even the harshest critics.  他令人释然的坦诚甚至赢得了最严厉批评者的好感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 endearing</td><td>/ɪnˈdɪrɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人喜爱的</td><td>an endearing habit<br>讨人喜欢的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puppy's clumsy walk was oddly endearing to everyone.  小狗笨拙的走姿让所有人莫名喜爱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 winsome</td><td>/ˈwɪnsəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，漂亮的</td><td>a winsome smile<br>迷人的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her winsome smile made the shy children relax almost at once.  她迷人的微笑让害羞的孩子们几乎立刻放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 adulatory</del></td><td>/ˈædʒələˌtɔri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谄媚的，过分吹捧的</td><td>an adulatory review<br>吹捧的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine ran an adulatory profile of the young director.  杂志刊登了一篇吹捧那位年轻导演的报道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deferential</td><td>/ˌdefəˈrenʃəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恭敬的，谦让的</td><td>a deferential tone<br>恭敬的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young clerk was unfailingly deferential toward senior partners.  这位年轻职员对资深合伙人始终恭敬有礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fawning</td><td>/ˈfɔːnɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>讨好的，谄媚的</td><td>fawning praise<br>谄媚的赞美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters grew tired of the star's fawning entourage.  记者们厌倦了这位明星身边谄媚的随从。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flattering</td><td>/ˈflætərɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>奉承的；美化的</td><td>a flattering description<br>奉承美化的描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The portrait was flattering but hardly looked like him.  这幅画像很美化，却几乎不像他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 toady</td><td>/ˈtoʊdi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>马屁精；拍马屁</td><td>toady to the powerful<br>对权贵拍马屁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Junior officials toady to the powerful hoping for quick promotion.  下级官员对权贵拍马屁，希望快速升迁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insinuating</td><td>/ɪnˈsɪnjueɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>讨好的；含暗示的</td><td>an insinuating manner<br>曲意逢迎的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He asked about her salary in an insinuating whisper.  他用一种别有暗示的低语问起她的薪水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disagreeable</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈɡriːəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不快的；脾气坏的</td><td>a disagreeable task<br>令人不快的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cleaning the drains was a thoroughly disagreeable job.  清理下水道是件极其讨人厌的活儿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>inherent</b></td><td>/ɪnˈhɪrənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>内在的，本质的</td><td>an inherent risk<br>固有的风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is an inherent danger in climbing without a rope.  无绳攀岩存在内在的危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 constitutional</td><td>/ˌkɑːnstəˈtuːʃənəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>体质上的，内在固有的</td><td>a constitutional weakness<br>体质上的虚弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His shyness seems constitutional rather than a habit he learned.  他的害羞似乎是天性使然，而非后天习得的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elemental</td><td>/ˌeləˈmentəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>基本的，根本的</td><td>elemental human needs<br>人的基本需求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The story explores elemental fears of darkness and loss.  这个故事探讨对黑暗与失去的原始恐惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 essential</td><td>/ɪˈsenʃəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>本质的，必不可少的</td><td>an essential quality<br>本质的特性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Patience is an essential part of her character.  耐心是她性格中本质的一部分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inborn</td><td>/ˈɪnˌbɔːrn/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>天生的，先天的</td><td>an inborn talent<br>天生的才能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She seems to have an inborn sense of rhythm.  她似乎天生就有节奏感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ingrained</td><td>/ɪnˈɡreɪnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>本质的，根深蒂固的</td><td>deeply ingrained habits<br>根深蒂固的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Habits ingrained since childhood are extremely difficult to change in later life.  童年就已根深蒂固的习惯到了晚年极难改变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 innate</td><td>/ɪˈneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>天生的，固有的</td><td>an innate ability<br>天生的能力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children have an innate curiosity about the world.  儿童对世界有一种天生的好奇心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intrinsic</td><td>/ɪnˈtrɪnsɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固有的，内在的</td><td>intrinsic value<br>内在价值</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting has an intrinsic beauty independent of its price.  这幅画有着与价格无关的内在美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adventitious</td><td>/ˌædvenˈtɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>外来的，后天的，非内在的</td><td>an adventitious circumstance<br>偶然的外部情况</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her success was not adventitious but the result of years of work.  她的成功不是偶然外来的，而是多年努力的结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>inimical</b></td><td>/ɪˈnɪmɪkl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的；不友好的</td><td>inimical to free speech<br>有害于言论自由</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such heavy censorship is clearly inimical to free discussion among scholars.  如此严厉的审查显然有害于学者之间的自由讨论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antagonistic</td><td>/ænˌtæɡəˈnɪstɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>敌对的，对抗的</td><td>antagonistic to reform<br>反对改革的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union was openly antagonistic to the new management.  工会对新管理层公开敌对。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hostile</td><td>/ˈhɑːstəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>敌对的，怀有敌意的</td><td>a hostile reaction<br>充满敌意的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The audience turned hostile as soon as he raised taxes.  他一提加税，观众立刻变得充满敌意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 opposing</td><td>/əˈpoʊzɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>对立的，相反的</td><td>opposing views<br>对立的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two scholars hold sharply opposing views on the treaty.  两位学者对该条约持截然对立的观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfriendly</td><td>/ʌnˈfrendli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不友好的，冷淡的</td><td>an unfriendly stare<br>不友好的注视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The waiter gave us an unfriendly look and walked away.  服务员不友好地看了我们一眼便走开了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amiable</td><td>/ˈeɪmiəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好脾气的，友好易相处的</td><td>an amiable manner<br>和气友善的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our amiable guide answered the same question a dozen times.  我们和气的导游把同一个问题回答了十几遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amicable</td><td>/ˈæmɪkəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>友善的，和睦的</td><td>an amicable settlement<br>友好的和解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dispute ended in an amicable agreement between neighbors.  这场纠纷以邻里间的友好协议告终。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 friendly</td><td>/ˈfrendli/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>友好的，亲切的</td><td>a friendly welcome<br>友好的欢迎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The shopkeeper was friendly and remembered every regular customer.  店主很友好，记得每位常客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hospitable</td><td>/ˈhɑːspɪtəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情好客的；宜于生长的</td><td>hospitable to strangers<br>对陌生人热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers were remarkably hospitable to the exhausted travelers.  村民对疲惫的旅人非常好客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>inimitable</b></td><td>/ɪˈnɪmɪtəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法仿效的，独特的</td><td>her inimitable style<br>她独一无二的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old pianist played the sonata with his own inimitable sense of rhythm.  这位老钢琴家以他独一无二的节奏感演奏了这首奏鸣曲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incomparable</td><td>/ɪnˈkɑːmpərəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无与伦比的</td><td>incomparable beauty<br>无与伦比的美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soprano's voice was incomparable among singers of her generation.  这位女高音的嗓音在同代歌手中无与伦比。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 matchless</td><td>/ˈmætʃləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无与匹敌的</td><td>matchless skill<br>无人能及的技艺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old craftsman carved wood with matchless precision.  这位老工匠雕刻木头的精准无人能及。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peerless</td><td>/ˈpɪrlɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无与匹敌的</td><td>a peerless performance<br>无与匹敌的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remains a peerless interpreter of Chopin's nocturnes.  她仍是肖邦夜曲无人能及的演绎者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unique</td><td>/juˈniːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>独一无二的，独特的</td><td>a unique opportunity<br>独一无二的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Each fingerprint is unique to the person who made it.  每个指纹都是其主人独有的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unparalleled</td><td>/ʌnˈpærəleld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无可比拟的</td><td>unparalleled success<br>无可比拟的成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city enjoyed unparalleled prosperity during the trade boom.  贸易繁荣期间这座城市享有无可比拟的富足。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ordinary</td><td>/ˈɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普通的，平常的</td><td>just an ordinary weekday<br>只是平常的工作日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked like an ordinary commuter waiting for the morning bus.  他看上去就像个等早班公交的普通上班族。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>iniquity</b></td><td>/ɪˈnɪkwəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>邪恶，不公正</td><td>a den of iniquity<br>罪恶的窝点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel exposes the iniquity of a legal system that protects only the rich.  这部小说揭露了只保护富人的司法制度的不公。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 corruption</td><td>/kəˈrʌpʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>腐败，贪污</td><td>fight against corruption<br>反腐斗争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investigators uncovered widespread corruption in the licensing office.  调查人员揭露了发证部门的普遍腐败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 depravity</td><td>/dɪˈprævəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>道德败坏</td><td>sink into utter depravity<br>堕入彻底的道德败坏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The trial revealed a level of depravity that shocked the small town.  庭审揭露出的道德败坏程度震惊了这个小镇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debauchery</td><td>/dəˈbɔːtʃəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>放荡，纵欲</td><td>a life of debauchery<br>放荡的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wasted his inheritance on years of reckless debauchery.  他把遗产浪费在多年放荡纵欲上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evil</td><td>/ˈiːvəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>邪恶；邪恶的</td><td>the lesser evil<br>较小的祸害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel examines how ordinary people come to commit evil.  这部小说探讨普通人如何走向作恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infamy</td><td>/ˈɪnfəmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶名，臭名；恶行</td><td>a day of infamy<br>耻辱的一天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His name lives on in infamy after the scandal.  丑闻之后他的名字以恶名流传下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sin</td><td>/ˈsɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>罪过，过失</td><td>confess one's sins<br>承认自己的罪过</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In her view, wasting food is a serious sin.  在她看来，浪费食物是严重的过错。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfairness</td><td>/ʌnˈfernəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不公平，不公正</td><td>the unfairness of the system<br>制度的不公</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students complained about the unfairness of the grading policy.  学生们抱怨评分政策不公平。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wickedness</td><td>/ˈwɪkədnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>邪恶，恶劣</td><td>human wickedness<br>人性的邪恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The preacher warned his listeners against greed and wickedness.  牧师警告听众远离贪婪与邪恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 integrity</td><td>/ɪnˈteɡrəti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直；完整性</td><td>a man of integrity<br>正直的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone respected the judge as a man of unshakable integrity and courage.  所有人都尊敬这位法官，视他为正直不屈且勇气可嘉的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rectitude</td><td>/ˈrektɪtuːd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直；品行端正</td><td>moral rectitude<br>道德上的正直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old judge was admired for his moral rectitude and quiet courage.  这位老法官因道德上的正直和沉静的勇气而受人敬仰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>initiate</b></td><td>/ɪˈnɪʃieɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>创始，发起</td><td>initiate a reform program<br>发起一项改革计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new governor plans to initiate a sweeping reform of rural education.  新州长计划发起一项农村教育的全面改革。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 begin</td><td>/bɪˈɡɪn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>开始，着手</td><td>begin work on the project<br>开始着手这个项目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We will begin the renovation as soon as the permit arrives.  许可一到我们就开始装修。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 start</td><td>/ˈstɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>开始，开端</td><td>from the start<br>从一开始</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the start it was clear the schedule was too tight.  从一开始就明显看出日程太紧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commence</td><td>/kəˈmens/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>开始，着手（正式）</td><td>commence legal proceedings<br>开始法律诉讼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Construction will commence next month if funding is approved.  若资金获批，工程将于下月开始。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inaugurate</td><td>/ɪˈnɔːɡjəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>开始</td><td>inaugurate a new era<br>开创一个新时代</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty inaugurated a long period of peace between the two nations.  这项条约开启了两国之间长期的和平。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 introduce</td><td>/ˌɪntrəˈduːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引入，开始实行；介绍</td><td>introduce a new policy<br>推行一项新政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city introduced free bus travel for elderly residents.  该市开始为老年居民提供免费公交。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 launch</td><td>/ˈlɔːntʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>发起，开始；发射</td><td>launch a new campaign<br>发起新的活动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company plans to launch its cheapest model this autumn.  公司计划今秋推出其最便宜的型号。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 terminate</td><td>/ˈtɜːrməˌneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>终止，结束</td><td>terminate the agreement<br>终止协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The club terminated his membership without any explanation.  俱乐部未加解释就终止了他的会员资格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>inkling</b></td><td>/ˈɪŋklɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>轻微暗示，小提示</td><td>have no inkling of<br>对…毫不知情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We had no inkling of their engagement until the invitations finally arrived.  直到请帖终于寄到，我们才知道他们订婚的事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clue</td><td>/ˈkluː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>线索，提示</td><td>an important clue<br>重要的线索</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Detectives found a single clue in the abandoned car.  侦探在废弃汽车里找到唯一一条线索。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cue</td><td>/ˈkjuː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>提示，暗示</td><td>take a cue from<br>从…得到提示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The actor waited offstage for his cue to enter.  演员在台侧等待上场的提示。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hint</td><td>/ˈhɪnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暗示，提示</td><td>drop a hint<br>透露一点暗示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave me a subtle hint about the surprise party.  她微妙地暗示了那个惊喜派对。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>innocuous</b></td><td>/ɪˈnɑːkjuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的；不会引起敌意的</td><td>an innocuous question<br>一个无恶意的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 What seemed an innocuous question turned out to reveal her deepest secret.  看似无恶意的问题最终却揭露了她最深的秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 anodyne</td><td>/ˈænədaɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>anodyne remarks<br>不痛不痒的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The spokesman gave anodyne answers that offended nobody at all.  发言人给出的回答不痛不痒，谁也没得罪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 benign</td><td>/bɪˈnaɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>a benign influence<br>有益无害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in policy proved benign for most small farmers.  事实证明，政策变化对大多数小农户是无害的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harmless</td><td>/ˈhɑːrmləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，不会造成伤害的</td><td>a harmless little joke<br>无伤大雅的玩笑</td></tr>
